# BURKINA FASO 4TH REPUBLIC

BURKINA FASO 4TH REPUBLIC

------------ SEVENTH LEGISLATURE

UNITY-PROGRESS-JUSTICE

NATIONAL ASSEMBLY

LAW NO. 025-2018/AN

ESTABLISHING THE PENAL CODE

THE NATIONAL ASSEMBLY

Having regard to the Constitution;

Having regard to resolution no. 001-2015/AN of December 30, 2015 validating the mandate of deputies; deliberated in its session of May 31, 2018 and adopted the law which reads as follows:

## BOOK I: GENERAL PROVISIONS

#### TITLE I: CRIMINAL LAW

##### CHAPTER 1: GENERAL PRINCIPLES

###### Article 111-1:

No offense may be punished and no penalty pronounced unless legally provided for.

###### Article 111-2:

Criminal law shall be strictly construed.

Only acts constituting an offense on the date they were committed are punishable.

###### Article 111-3:

No one may be declared criminally liable and consequently incur a sanction unless he or she has committed an offense.

###### Article 111-4:

There is no crime or misdemeanor without intent to commit it. However, when the law so provides, there is a misdemeanor in cases of deliberate endangerment of another person and in cases of recklessness, negligence or failure to comply with a duty of care or safety provided for by law or regulation.

In the cases provided for in the preceding paragraph, natural persons who have not directly caused the damage, but who have created or contributed to creating the situation that enabled the damage to occur or who have not taken measures to prevent it, are criminally liable if it is established that they have either deliberately violated a particular duty of care or safety provided for by law or regulation, or committed a characterized fault that exposed others to a serious risk of which they could not have been unaware.

###### Article 111-5:

Every person is entitled, in full equality, to a fair and public hearing by an independent and impartial tribunal, which will decide either on his or her rights and obligations or on the merits of any criminal charge against him or her.

###### Article 111-6:

Every person accused of committing an offense is presumed innocent until his or her guilt has been legally established. No one may be found guilty of an offense or sentenced to a penalty except by decision of a competent court.

###### Article 111-7:

Treaties, agreements or conventions duly ratified and published shall prevail over domestic criminal provisions.

###### Article 111-8:

There is a concurrence of offenses when an offense is committed by a person before he or she has been definitively convicted of another offense.

In the event of conviction of several crimes or misdemeanors, only the most severe penalty shall be pronounced.

There is a cumulation of penalties in cases of actual concurrence between violations, between misdemeanors and unrelated violations or between crimes and unrelated violations.

###### Article 111-9:

When an individual is the subject of several convictions for crimes or misdemeanors resulting from separate prosecutions before any court, merger of sentences must be pronounced.

When a principal penalty is the subject of clemency, the penalty resulting from the commutation and not the initially pronounced penalty must be taken into account for the purposes of the merger of sentences.

##### CHAPTER 2: APPLICATION OF CRIMINAL LAW OVER TIME

###### Article 112-1:

A law that removes the punishable nature of an act has retroactive effect. It terminates any ongoing prosecution as well as execution of the pronounced penalty.

A law that reduces a penalty has retroactive effect. It applies to offenses committed before its entry into force and which have not resulted in a conviction that has become final.

A law that makes an act punishable or that increases a penalty has no retroactive effect.

##### CHAPTER 3: APPLICATION OF CRIMINAL LAW IN SPACE

###### Article 113-1:

Burkinabe criminal law applies to any offense committed on the national territory regardless of the nationality of its perpetrator.

Criminal law also applies to offenses committed by a national or against a national outside the national territory when the acts are punished by the legislation of the country where they were committed. Prosecution in this case must be preceded by a complaint from the victim or an official denunciation by the authority of the country where the acts were committed.

Prosecution ceases in the case where the person proves having been definitively tried abroad for the same acts, and in the event of conviction, when the penalty has been executed or is time-barred.

Burkinabe criminal law is also applicable to offenses committed on board aircraft registered in Burkina Faso, or aircraft leased without crew and operated by persons meeting the conditions to be an aircraft owner in Burkina Faso, or against persons on board. It is solely applicable to offenses committed on board Burkinabe military aircraft, or against persons on board such aircraft.

It is also applicable to offenses committed on board a vessel registered under Burkinabe law or in respect of which a permit or identification number has been issued in accordance with that law, or on board a vessel registered abroad and belonging to the Burkinabe State or against persons on board such vessels.

For the application of this article, Burkinabe courts have jurisdiction.

###### Article 113-2:

When the extradition, expulsion or refoulement of a person is refused by the Burkinabe authorities to a State where that person risks being subjected to torture or similar practices, Burkinabe courts have jurisdiction to try the person provided that the acts that are the subject of the request for surrender are provided for and punished by the legislation in force in Burkina Faso or if they constitute an international crime.

#### TITLE II: OFFENSE AND ATTEMPT

##### CHAPTER 1: CLASSIFICATION OF OFFENSES

###### Article 121-1:

Crimes are offenses punishable by life imprisonment or imprisonment exceeding ten years.

Misdemeanors are offenses punishable by imprisonment of at least thirty days and not exceeding ten years and/or punishable by a fine exceeding two hundred thousand (200,000) CFA francs.

Violations are offenses punishable by a fine not exceeding two hundred thousand (200,000) CFA francs.

##### CHAPTER 2: ATTEMPT

###### Article 122-1:

An attempt consists in the undertaking to commit a crime or misdemeanor, manifested by unequivocal acts tending toward its execution, if such acts were suspended or failed to have their effect only due to circumstances independent of the will of their perpetrator.

An attempt is punishable even when the desired objective could not be achieved due to circumstances unknown to the perpetrator.

###### Article 122-2:

An attempt to commit a crime is always punishable.

An attempt to commit a misdemeanor is punishable only in cases provided for by law.

An attempt to commit a violation is not punishable.

###### Article 122-3:

A preparatory act does not constitute an offense except under contrary legal provisions.

###### Article 122-4:

The penalty applicable to an attempt is that of the offense itself.

#### TITLE III: CRIMINAL LIABILITY

##### CHAPTER 1: PUNISHABLE PERSONS

###### Article 131-1:

The age of criminal majority is set at eighteen years.

It is assessed on the day of the commission of the acts.

The age of the minor is determined by the production of birth certificates, declaratory judgments or any other documents corroborated by medical expertise. In case of disagreement as to the determination of age, the competent court seised has sovereign discretion.

If the documents produced specify only the year of birth, it shall be considered as having occurred on the thirty-first of December of said year. If the month is specified, the birth shall be considered as having occurred on the last day of said month.

###### Article 131-2:

Any natural person who, personally and principally, accomplishes the constituent elements of an offense by commission or omission or who is at the origin of such acts is an author or co-author.

Any legal person with a civil, commercial, industrial or financial purpose in whose name and interest acts of execution or abstention constituting an offense have been accomplished by the deliberate will of its organs or its representative, in the exercise of their function, is also an author or co-author.

###### Article 131-3:

The State and its subdivisions are also criminally liable for offenses committed on their behalf, by their organs or representatives, in the exercise of their function.

Local authorities and their groupings are criminally liable only for offenses committed in the exercise of activities likely to be the subject of public service delegation agreements. The criminal liability of legal persons, whether private or public law, does not exclude that of natural persons who are authors or accomplices of the same acts, unless the law provides otherwise.

###### Article 131-4:

Is an accomplice to an act qualified as a crime or misdemeanor:

- anyone who procures weapons, instruments or any other means that were used in the action while knowing that they were to be used therein;
- anyone who knowingly prepared or facilitated the commission of the action, aided or assisted the author or authors of the acts;
- anyone who by gift, promise, threat, order, abuse of authority or power provoked the commission of an offense or gave instructions to commit it;
- anyone who, knowing the criminal conduct of wrongdoers exercising acts of brigandage or violence against the security of the State, public peace, persons or property, habitually provides them with lodging, place of retreat or meeting;
- anyone who, having knowledge of a crime or misdemeanor already attempted or committed, did not, when it was still possible to prevent or limit its effects or when it could be thought that the guilty parties or one of them would commit new crimes that a denunciation can prevent, immediately warn the administrative or judicial authorities.

Excluded from the cases referred to in bullets 4 and 5 of this article are the spouse, relatives or relatives by marriage of the author of the crime or misdemeanor up to and including the fourth degree.

###### Article 131-5:

Accomplices to a crime or misdemeanor are punished like the authors of the crime or misdemeanor unless the law provides otherwise.

###### Article 131-6:

The authors, co-authors and accomplices of a crime or misdemeanor or of an attempted crime or misdemeanor are also liable for any other offense whose commission or attempt is a foreseeable consequence of the offense.

###### Article 131-7:

Anyone who causes a non-punishable person to commit an offense is liable to the penalties of the offense committed.

###### Article 131-8:

Anyone who incites the commission of a crime or misdemeanor is punished with the penalties provided for the offense even if it was not committed due to the voluntary abstention of the person who was to commit it.

##### CHAPTER 2: GROUNDS FOR CRIMINAL IRRESPONSIBILITY

###### Section 1: Justifying circumstances

###### Article 132-1:

A person who accomplishes an act prescribed or authorized by legislative or regulatory provisions is not criminally liable. A person who accomplishes an act ordered by legitimate authority is not criminally liable, unless the act is manifestly illegal.

###### Article 132-2:

A person who, faced with an unjustified attack against themselves or others, accomplishes, at the same time, an act required by the necessity of self-defense or defense of others is not criminally liable, unless there is disproportion between the means of defense employed and the seriousness of the attack.

A person who, to interrupt the execution of a crime or misdemeanor against property, accomplishes an act of defense, other than voluntary homicide, when this act is strictly necessary for the purpose pursued provided that the means employed are proportionate to the seriousness of the offense, is not criminally liable.

Acts committed are notably required by the immediate necessity of self-defense:

‑ to repel, at night, entry by climbing, breaking in, violence or ruse into a house, an inhabited apartment or their outbuildings or into any other inhabited place;

‑ to defend oneself against the perpetrators of thefts or looting executed with violence.

###### Article 132-3:

A person who, faced with an actual or imminent danger that threatens themselves, others or property, accomplishes an act necessary to safeguard the person or property is not criminally liable, unless there is disproportion between the means employed and the seriousness of the threat.

###### Section 2: Grounds for non-imputability

###### Article 132-4:

There is no crime, no misdemeanor, no petty offense when the author was in a state of insanity at the time of the action. When the court considers that the mental state of the insane person could compromise public order or the safety of persons, it may order their confinement and care in a specialized center. Care costs are borne in accordance with the health legislation in force.

There is no crime, no misdemeanor, no petty offense when the author acted under the influence of a force or constraint which they could not resist.

###### Article 132-5:

The person who was affected, at the time of the acts, by a psychic or neuropsychic disorder having altered their judgment or hindered the control of their acts remains punishable; however, the court takes this circumstance into account when determining the sentence and setting its regime.

###### Article 132-6:

When a public service of the State or a public authority is at the origin of an error of law, a person who justifies having believed, by this error which they were not able to avoid, that they could legitimately accomplish the act is not criminally liable.

###### Article 132-7:

The age of criminal responsibility is set at thirteen years.

There is no crime, no misdemeanor, no petty offense when the author of the offense was less than thirteen years old, on the date of the commission of the acts.

The minor under thirteen years old can only be subject to educational and safety measures.

Other categories of minors criminally responsible for crimes, misdemeanors or petty offenses are dealt with under conditions set by a specific law which determines the measures of protection, assistance, supervision and education.

###### Article 132-8:

In cases of non-imputability, the courts seised may order all restitutions and pronounce all reparations in accordance with the provisions of the civil code.

## BOOK II: PENALTIES, SAFETY MEASURES AND EDUCATIONAL

MEASURES

#### TITLE I: PENALTIES

##### CHAPTER 1: GENERAL PROVISIONS

###### Article 211-1:

The judge may always impose a firm sentence or a suspended sentence or a mixed sentence.

The suspension consists of a partial or total exemption from the execution of a sentence of imprisonment or fine.

The suspension is not applicable to sentences of imprisonment imposed for a period exceeding five years.

The suspension is revocable under the conditions governed by the code of criminal procedure.

The mixed sentence consists of imposing a sentence of imprisonment or fine of which one part is firm and the other is suspended.

The conviction to the penalties provided by law is always imposed without prejudice to restitution and damages that may be owed to the parties.

##### CHAPTER 2: PENALTIES IN CRIMINAL MATTERS

###### Section 1: Penalties applicable to adults

###### Article 212-1:

Penalties in criminal matters are:

˗ life imprisonment;

˗ imprisonment for a fixed term;

˗ fine for legal persons;

˗ dissolution for legal persons.

Criminal penalties are not exclusive of one or more legally provided complementary penalties or a fine.

The competent jurisdiction may also order the destruction of the thing which served or was intended to commit the offense or the thing which is the product thereof.

###### Article 212-2:

The conviction to the penalty of imprisonment for a fixed term, in criminal matters, is from more than ten years up to life imprisonment.

###### Article 212-3:

The conviction to a criminal penalty may entail civic degradation. The civic degradation imposed runs from the day the conviction became final.

###### Article 212-4:

Civic degradation consists of:

˗ the removal and exclusion of the convicted from all functions, employments or public offices;

˗ the deprivation of the right to vote, eligibility and in general of all civic and political rights and the right to wear a decoration;

˗ the incapacity to be an expert, witness in deeds and to testify in court except to provide simple information;

˗ the incapacity to be part of any family council, and to be guardian, curator, deputy guardian or judicial advisor, except for one's own children and upon the favorable opinion of the family;

˗ the deprivation of the right to bear arms, to serve in the national armies, to teach or to direct an educational or training establishment, to be employed in an educational institution, as a teacher or supervisor.

###### Article 212-5:

A person sentenced to life imprisonment may not dispose of his property, in whole or in part, either by inter vivos donation or by will, nor receive it on this ground, except for maintenance. Any will made by him prior to his adversarial conviction, which has become final, is void. The above provisions are not applicable to the person convicted in absentia. A person sentenced to life imprisonment may be relieved of all or part of the incapacities contained in the preceding paragraph. He may be granted the exercise, in the place of execution of the sentence, of civil rights or some of these rights, of which he has been deprived by his state of legal prohibition. Acts performed by the convicted person in the place of execution of the sentence may not bind the property he possessed on the day of his conviction, or which devolved to him gratuitously since that time.

###### Article 212-6:

In all cases where a conviction is imposed for a crime against the external security of the State, committed in time of war, the competent jurisdictions order the confiscation, for the benefit of the nation, of all present property of the convicted person, of whatever nature, movable, immovable, divided or undivided, according to the modalities provided in articles 212-7 and 212-8 below.

###### Article 212-7:

If the convicted person is married, the confiscation applies only to the share of the convicted person in the partition of the community property, or undivided property between his spouse and him. If he has descendants or ascendants, the confiscation applies only to the disposable portion. Where appropriate, partition or sale by auction is carried out according to the rules applicable in matters of succession.

###### Article 212-8:

The alienation of confiscated property is pursued by the National Agency for the Management and Recovery of Seized or Confiscated Assets in the forms prescribed for the sale of State property.

Property devolved to the State by the effect of confiscation remains encumbered, up to their value, with legitimate debts prior to the conviction.

###### Section 2: Penalties applicable to minors

###### Article 212-9:

Without prejudice to the application of the provisions of this chapter which are not contrary to this article, a minor over thirteen years of age, found guilty of a crime may be sentenced, by reasoned decision, to a custodial sentence.

When the excuse of minority is accepted, the sentence imposed may not exceed half the sentence provided for adults. In any event, it may not exceed fifteen years.

The measures and sanctions provided for in article 213-8 may, where applicable, be imposed on a minor found guilty of a crime.

##### CHAPTER 3: PENALTIES IN CORRECTIONAL MATTERS

###### Section 1: Penalties applicable to adults

###### Article 213-1:

Penalties in correctional matters are:

˗ imprisonment for a fixed term;

˗ fine;

˗ community service;

˗ temporary prohibition;

˗ permanent prohibition;

˗ dissolution for legal persons.

The competent jurisdiction may also order the confiscation and/or destruction of the thing which served or was intended to commit the offense or the thing which is the product thereof.

In all cases, correctional penalties are not exclusive of one or more legally provided complementary penalties.

###### Article 213-2:

Dissolution may be imposed only:

˗ when the legal person is legally created;

˗ when it concerns a crime or an offense punishable by a sentence of imprisonment greater than or equal to three years for natural persons;

˗ when the legal person has been diverted from its purpose to commit the incriminated acts.

###### Article 213-3:

The duration of the sentence of imprisonment, in correctional matters, is at least thirty days and at most ten years, except in cases where the law has determined other limits.

In the sentence of imprisonment, each day counts as twenty-four hours and each month as thirty days.

###### Article 213-4:

Community service is a penalty that the correctional jurisdiction may impose as a principal penalty when an offense is punishable by a sentence of imprisonment. It consists of having the consenting convicted person perform, for a period provided by law, unpaid community service for the benefit of a legal person under public law or a legally recognized non-profit association.

It may not be imposed cumulatively with one of the other penalties provided for in article 213-1 above.

Community service may not be imposed on the defendant who refuses it or who is not present at the hearing. The president of the court, before the pronouncement of the judgment, informs the defendant of his right to refuse the performance of community service and receives his response.

The quantum and modalities of implementation of community service are determined by the law on the administration of community service.

###### Article 213-5 :

Community service sentences are expressed in hours or days. The court that pronounces the community service sentence sets the period during which the community service must be completed within a limit of twenty-four months. The period ends upon completion of all the community service; it may be temporarily suspended for serious medical, family, professional or social reasons. This period is suspended during the time when the convicted person is placed in pre-trial detention, serves a custodial sentence or fulfills national service obligations. The methods of execution of the obligation to perform community service and the suspension of the period provided for in the preceding paragraph are decided by the sentence enforcement judge.

###### Article 213-6 :

Correctional courts may, in certain cases, prohibit, in whole or in part, the exercise of the following civic, civil and family rights: ˗ the right to vote;

˗ the right to stand for election;

˗ the right to be called or appointed to public functions or to positions in the administration, or to exercise these functions or positions;

˗ the right to bear arms;

˗ the right to vote and suffrage in family deliberations;

˗ the right to be a guardian, curator, except in the case of one's own children and only on the advice of the family;

˗ the right to be an expert or used as a witness in legal acts;

˗ the right to testify in court, other than to make simple declarations.

###### Article 213-7 :

Correctional courts only pronounce the prohibition mentioned in the preceding article when it is authorized or ordered by a particular provision of law.

###### Section 2 : Penalties applicable to minors

###### Article 213-8 :

Without prejudice to the application of the provisions of this chapter that are not contrary to this article, a minor against whom the charge of an offense is established may be sentenced by reasoned decision to one or more of the following measures:

˗ admonishment;

˗ reprimand;

˗ return to his parents, to his extended family, to his legal representatives, to a trustworthy person;

˗ placement in an institution, a public or private establishment authorized for education or vocational training;

˗ placement in an institution or a public or private establishment specialized in the reintegration of children in conflict with the law through education or vocational training;

˗ placement in a medical or medico-educational establishment, if necessary.

A minor over the age of thirteen may also be sentenced to a fine or to fixed-term imprisonment.

A minor over the age of sixteen may be sentenced to community service, under the conditions provided for in this code.

##### CHAPTER 4 : OTHER PENALTIES FOR CRIMES AND OFFENSES

###### Article 214-1 :

When the law so provides, a legal person, prosecuted for a crime or an offense, may, in addition to the principal penalty, be sanctioned with one or more of the following penalties:

˗ prohibition, permanently or for a period of up to five years, from exercising directly or indirectly one or more professional or social activities;

˗ placement, for a period of up to five years, under judicial supervision;

˗ permanent or for a period of up to five years closure of all establishments or of one or more establishments of the enterprise that were used to commit the incriminated acts;

˗ exclusion from public contracts and delegations of public services permanently or for a period of up to five years;

˗ prohibition, permanently or for a period of up to five years, from making a public offering of financial securities or from having its financial securities admitted to trading on a regulated market;

˗ prohibition, for a period of up to five years, from issuing checks other than those that allow the withdrawal of funds by the drawer from the drawee or those that are certified or from using payment cards;

˗ the penalty of confiscation of all or part of its property;

˗ publication of the decision pronounced, either in the written press, or by any means of communication to the public including electronically or by posting thereof.

###### Article 214-2 :

Prohibition from residence, fine and special confiscation, either of the corpus delicti, when the property belongs to the convicted person, or of things produced by the offense, or of those that were used or that were intended to commit it, are penalties common to criminal and correctional matters.

###### Article 214-3 :

The duration of any custodial sentence runs from the date on which the conviction decision became final.

###### Article 214-4 :

In case of conviction to a fixed-term imprisonment sentence, pre-trial detention is deducted day for day from the duration of the sentence pronounced by the judgment or conviction order.

###### Article 214-5 :

Imprisonment sentences are executed in the penitentiary establishments provided for this purpose.

###### Article 214-6 :

The prohibition from exercising a public function or a professional or social activity is either permanent or temporary. In the latter case, it may not exceed ten years.

This prohibition is not applicable to the exercise of an elective mandate or union responsibility. It is also not applicable in matters of press offenses.

###### Article 214-7 :

The penalty of exclusion from public contracts and delegations of public service entails the prohibition from participating, directly or indirectly, in any contract concluded by the State and its public establishments, local authorities, their groups and their public establishments, as well as by enterprises granted or controlled by the State or by local authorities or their groups.

The exclusion penalty is permanent or temporary. In the latter case, it may not exceed five years.

###### Article 214-8 :

The penalty of closure of an establishment entails the prohibition from exercising the activity in connection with which the offense was committed.

###### Article 214-9 :

Is punished by imprisonment from one year to three years and a fine from five hundred thousand (500,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who violates the penalty of prohibition from exercising a commercial or industrial profession, from directing, administering, managing or controlling in any capacity whatsoever, directly or indirectly, on one's own account or on behalf of others, a commercial or industrial enterprise or a commercial company, pronounced against them.

###### Article 214-10 :

Prohibition from residence consists of the prohibition made to a convicted person from appearing in certain places.

It includes, in addition, measures of surveillance and assistance. Its duration is from two years to ten years in correctional matters, from eleven years to thirty years in criminal matters.

It may be pronounced against:

˗ anyone convicted to imprisonment for a crime;

˗ anyone convicted for a crime or offense against the internal or external security of the State;

˗ anyone who, having been convicted to a sentence exceeding one year of imprisonment, has, within a period of five years after the expiration of this sentence or its prescription, been convicted to a sentence equal to or exceeding one year of imprisonment.

###### Article 214-11 :

Any person sentenced to life imprisonment who obtains a commutation or remission of sentence is, unless otherwise provided by the pardon decision, subject by operation of law to residence prohibition for five years.

The same applies to any person sentenced to life imprisonment whose sentence is time-barred.

###### Article 214-12 :

Territory prohibition may be pronounced for a period greater than or equal to ten years or permanently, against any foreigner guilty of a crime or misdemeanor.

The convicted person is returned to the border by operation of law, upon expiration of their imprisonment sentence, where applicable.

###### Article 214-13 :

The list of prohibited places is fixed by the minister in charge of territorial administration, by individual order taken jointly with the minister in charge of justice.

The same order determines the surveillance and assistance measures to which the convicted person may be subject.

###### Article 214-14 :

The prohibition order is notified to the convicted person who receives, in addition to an anthropometric notebook, the legal identity card. The decisions or orders taken in application of Article 214-13 above are also notified to them.

If notification of the prohibition order was made to the convicted person before their final or conditional release, the prohibition runs from the date of that release. However, in case of revocation of conditional release, the prohibition is suspended during the time of the new incarceration. The same applies in case of detention for any other cause.

If the prohibition order could not be notified to them before their release, the convicted person must, at that time, inform the director or chief guard of the penitentiary establishment where they were detained of the place where they intend to establish their residence. They are further required, during the three months following their release, to notify them of any change of that residence, and to respond to the summons sent to them by the administrative authority for notification of the prohibition order.

If they fulfill these obligations, the prohibition runs from the date of release; otherwise, it only takes effect from the day when notification of the prohibition order could be made to them.

If no custodial sentence without suspension has been pronounced or if that sentence has expired, notification of the prohibition order is made to the convicted person as soon as the judgment or decision imposing the residence prohibition sentence has become final; the prohibition runs from the day when the judgment or decision acquired that character.

In the case of a sentence of life imprisonment, the residence prohibition takes effect from the day when the limitation period is completed.

###### Article 214-15 :

Any person subject to residence prohibition who, in violation of the order notified to them, appears in a place prohibited to them is punished by imprisonment of three months to three years and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.

Anyone who evades the surveillance measures prescribed by the order notified to them, or who fails to respond to the summons sent to them by the administrative authority for notification of the prohibition order in the case provided for in paragraph 3 of Article 214-14 above, is punished by the same penalties.

###### Article 214-16 :

Joint orders taken by the ministers in charge of justice and territorial administration determine the conditions for application of Articles 214-10, 214-12, and 214-13 above.

They fix, in particular:

˗ the surveillance and assistance measures that may be prescribed in application of Articles 214-10 and 214-13 above;

˗ the conditions for establishment and delivery and the modalities of the documents provided for in Article 214-14, paragraph 1 above;

˗ the entries and endorsements to be made on the anthropometric notebook, the form of notifications of the orders provided for in Articles 214-13 and 214-14 and of the summons provided for in Article 214-14 paragraph 3 above.

###### Article 214-17 :

In cases specifically provided by law, the courts seized may order the posting of their decision, the content of which is set in very conspicuous characters, in the places they indicate, at the expense of the convicted person.

Unless otherwise provided by law, this posting is ordered for a period that cannot exceed two months in matters of crimes or misdemeanors.

The removal, concealment and tearing, total or partial, of posters affixed in accordance with this article, carried out voluntarily by the convicted person, at their instigation or on their orders, are punished by imprisonment of one month to six months and a fine of two hundred and fifty thousand (250,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs. The posting is again carried out in full at the expense of the convicted person. The courts seized may order the dissemination of all or part of their decision and determine the newspaper(s) or audiovisual communication service responsible for this dissemination.

The posting or dissemination may include the name of the victim only with their consent or that of their legal representative.

###### Article 214-18 :

If there is cause for restitution, the offender may be sentenced, in addition, to the

### injured party, if the latter so requests, to damages the determination of which is

left to the appreciation of the court seized.

###### Article 214-19 :

The judge, for serious medical, family, professional or social reasons, may decide on the installment payment of the fine.

For the same reasons and in the case where the judgment did not provide for it, the same court may, upon application of the convicted person, order the installment payment of the fine.

In case of insufficient assets, restitutions and damages owed to the injured party take precedence over the fine.

###### Article 214-20 :

Execution of sentences to fines and costs owed to the Public Treasury may be pursued by way of constraint by body.

###### Article 214-21 :

When fines and costs are pronounced in favor of the State, if, after expiration of the criminal sentence, the imprisonment of the convicted person, for payment of these pecuniary sentences, has lasted a full year, they may, upon proof established by legal means of their absolute insolvency, obtain their provisional release.

The duration of imprisonment is reduced to six months if it concerns a misdemeanor; subject, in all cases, to resuming the constraint by body if some means of solvency arises for the convicted person.

###### Article 214-22 :

All persons convicted for the same crime or for the same misdemeanor are jointly and severally liable for fines, restitutions, damages and costs.

###### Article 214-23 :

The additional penalty of confiscation is incurred in the cases provided for by law or regulation. It is also incurred automatically for crimes and for offenses punishable by imprisonment for a term exceeding one year, with the exception of press offenses.

Confiscation applies to all movable or immovable property, whatever its nature, divided or undivided, which was used to commit the offense or which was intended to commit it, and of which the convicted person is the owner or, subject to the rights of the owner in good faith, of which he has free disposal. It also applies to all property which is the object or the direct or indirect proceeds of the offense, with the exception of property that may be returned to the victim. If the proceeds of the offense have been mixed with funds of lawful origin for the acquisition of one or more properties, confiscation may apply to such property only up to the estimated value of such proceeds.

Confiscation may also apply to any movable or immovable property defined by the law or regulation that punishes the offense.

If it is a crime or an offense punishable by at least five years of imprisonment and having procured a direct or indirect profit, confiscation also applies to movable or immovable property, whatever its nature, divided or undivided, belonging to the convicted person or, subject to the rights of the owner in good faith, of which he has free disposal, when neither the convicted person nor the owner, given the opportunity to explain the property whose confiscation is contemplated, have been able to justify its origin.

When the law that punishes the crime or offense so provides, confiscation may also apply to all or part of the property belonging to the convicted person or, subject to the rights of the owner in good faith, of which he has free disposal, whatever its nature, movable or immovable, divided or undivided.

Confiscation is mandatory for objects classified as dangerous or harmful by law or regulation, or whose possession is illegal, whether or not such property is the property of the convicted person.

The additional penalty of confiscation applies under the same conditions to all incorporeal rights, whatever their nature, divided or undivided. Confiscation may be ordered in value. Confiscation in value may be executed on all property, whatever its nature, belonging to the convicted person or of which he has free disposal, subject to the rights of the owner in good faith. For the recovery of the sum representing the value of the confiscated thing, the provisions relating to judicial constraint are applicable.

The confiscated thing is, unless a specific provision provides for its destruction or allocation, devolved to the State, but it remains encumbered, up to its value, by real rights lawfully constituted in favor of third parties.

When the confiscated thing is a vehicle that was not seized or impounded during the procedure, the convicted person must, upon the injunction made to him by the public prosecutor, hand over this vehicle to the service or body responsible for its destruction or disposal.

##### CHAPITRE 5 : DES PEINES EN MATIERE CONTRAVENTIONNELLE

###### Article 215-1 :

Penalties in matters of petty offenses are:

˗ the fine;

˗ additional penalties legally provided for in matters of petty offenses.

##### CHAPITRE 6 : DE LA PERSONNALISATION DES PEINES ET DES

CIRCONSTANCES AGGRAVANTES

###### Article 216-1 :

Within the limits set by law, the court pronounces the penalties and sets their regime according to the circumstances of the offense and the personality of its perpetrator.

Personal circumstances resulting in an exoneration of responsibility, an exemption, a mitigation or an aggravation of penalty have effect only with regard to the person who is the subject thereof.

###### Article 216-2 :

Organized gang, assembly, ambush, premeditation, breaking and entering, scaling, use of a weapon, use of a means of cryptology notably constitute aggravating circumstances in the cases provided for by this code.

The status of spouse, that of father or mother, that of cohabitant, that of child may also constitute aggravating circumstances.

Objective circumstances inherent to the offense which aggravate or diminish the penalties have effect with regard to the co-perpetrator or accomplice only if he could foresee them.

###### Article 216-3 :

Constitutes an organized gang within the meaning of the law, any group formed or any agreement established with a view to the preparation, characterized by one or more material facts, of one or more offenses.

###### Article 216-4 :

Constitutes an assembly within the meaning of the law, any collective action of at least two persons, occasional or fortuitous, on the occasion of the commission of one or more offenses.

###### Article 216-5 :

Premeditation consists of the design formed before the action to harm the person of a determined individual or even of one who will be found or encountered, even if such design is dependent on some circumstance or condition.

###### Article 216-6 :

Ambush consists of waiting, for a certain time in one or various places, for an individual either to put him to death or to exercise acts of violence upon him.

###### Article 216-7 :

Breaking and entering consists of the forcing, damaging or destruction of any closing device or any kind of enclosure.

Assimilated to breaking and entering is the use of false keys, improperly obtained keys or any instrument that can be fraudulently used to operate a closing device without forcing or damaging it.

###### Article 216-8 :

Scaling is the act of entering any place, either over an element of enclosure, or through any opening not intended to serve as an entrance.

###### Article 216-9 :

Is a weapon, any object or device designed or intended to kill, injure, strike, neutralize or to cause incapacity.

Is also considered as a weapon for the application of this code, in addition to firearms and objects considered as weapons by the texts in force, any object, instrument, tool or utensil that is sharp, piercing or blunt, hand-held or projectile.

Scissors, pocket knives and simple canes are deemed weapons only if they are used to kill, injure, strike or threaten.

Is assimilated to a weapon any object which, presenting with the weapon defined in paragraph 1 a resemblance liable to create confusion, is used to threaten to kill or injure or is intended, by the person carrying it, to threaten to kill or injure.

The use of an animal to kill, injure or threaten is assimilated to the use of a weapon.

###### Article 216-10 :

A cryptology means is any hardware or software designed or modified to transform data, whether information or signals, using secret protocols or to perform the reverse operation with or without secret protocols.

The primary purpose of these cryptology means is to guarantee the security of data storage or transmission, by ensuring their confidentiality, authentication or integrity control. A cryptology service is any operation aimed at implementing, on behalf of others, cryptology means.

When a cryptology means has been used to prepare or commit a crime or an offense, or to facilitate the preparation or commission thereof, the maximum custodial sentence incurred is increased as follows: ˗ it is raised to life imprisonment when the offense is punishable by twenty years' imprisonment;

˗ it is raised to twenty years' imprisonment when the offense is punishable by less than twenty years and more than ten years' imprisonment;

˗ it is raised to ten years' imprisonment when the offense is punishable by more than three years and less than ten years' imprisonment;

˗ it is doubled when the offense is punishable by three years' imprisonment or less.

The provisions of this article are however not applicable to the perpetrator or accomplice of the offense who, at the request of the judicial or administrative authorities, has provided them with the plaintext version of the encrypted messages as well as the secret protocols necessary for decryption.

###### Article 216-11 :

When the cases are respectively provided for by law or regulation, the penalties incurred for a crime, an offense or a minor offense are aggravated when the infraction is committed by the spouse or cohabitant. The aggravating circumstance provided for in the first paragraph is also established when the acts are committed by the former spouse, the former cohabitant. The provisions of this paragraph apply when the infraction is committed by reason of the relationship that existed between the perpetrator of the acts and the victim.

In the cases provided for by law, the penalties incurred for a crime or an offense are aggravated when the infraction is committed by reason of the belonging or non-belonging, real or supposed, of the victim to a particular ethnic group, nation, race or religion.

The aggravating circumstance defined in the first paragraph is established when the infraction is preceded, accompanied or followed by words, writings, images, objects or acts of any nature affecting the honor or consideration of the victim or of a group of persons to which the victim belongs by reason of their belonging or non-belonging, real or supposed, to a particular ethnic group, nation, race or religion.

##### CHAPITRE 7 : DES EXCUSES ET DES CIRCONSTANCES ATTENUANTES

###### Article 217-1 :

No crime or offense may be excused, nor the penalty mitigated, except in the cases and circumstances where the law declares the act excusable or permits the application of a less severe penalty.

###### Article 217-2 :

Absolutory excuses ensure impunity for the perpetrator of the infraction; however, educational or security measures may be applied to them. Attenuating excuses ensure a moderation of the penalty for the guilty party. The minority of the perpetrator at the time of the acts is an attenuating excuse. The court seized of the merits may dismiss the excuse of minority for minors aged sixteen or older.

###### Article 217-3 :

When the excuse is admitted by the court seized, the applicable penalties are as follows:

˗ imprisonment of one year to five years, if the penalty incurred is life imprisonment;

˗ imprisonment of six months to two years, if the penalty incurred is imprisonment of five years to ten years or eleven years to twenty-one years; ˗ imprisonment of thirty days to six months, if the penalty incurred is imprisonment of two months to five years.

###### Article 217-4 :

Unless otherwise provided by law, if the court seized recognizes by reasoned decision circumstances for the guilty party which, without being excuses, are nevertheless of a nature to mitigate their criminal or delictual responsibility, the penalties are modified as follows:

˗ imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years, if the penalty incurred is life imprisonment;

˗ imprisonment of five years to ten years, if the penalty incurred is imprisonment of eleven to thirty years;

˗ imprisonment of two years to five years, if the penalty incurred is imprisonment of five years to ten years.

In correctional matters, the court that recognizes mitigating circumstances is authorized to reduce the imprisonment and the fine even below simple police penalties.

If the law provides for the cumulative application of imprisonment and a fine, the court may impose both penalties by reducing them or by reducing only one of them. If the law provides for a penalty of imprisonment alone, the court may substitute a fine therefor; the substitute fine is five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) CFA francs.

##### CHAPITRE 8 : DE LA RECIDIVE

###### Article 218-1 :

In cases limitatively enumerated by law, the penalties incurred for crimes and offenses are aggravated in case of recidivism.

###### Article 218-2 :

In criminal and delictual matters, is a recidivist whoever, after having been definitively convicted for a first infraction by a national or foreign court, provided that the infraction having motivated the conviction abroad is also an infraction under national law, commits a second crime or a second offense independent of the first infraction.

###### Article 218-3 :

May be sentenced to double the penalties provided for the second infraction whoever having:

˗ already been definitively convicted for a crime, commits a new crime;

˗ already been definitively convicted for a crime, commits within the period of five years from the expiration or prescription of the sentence, an intentional offense;

˗ already been definitively convicted for an intentional offense, commits within the period of five years, from the expiration or prescription of the sentence, a crime;

˗ already been definitively convicted for an offense, commits within the period of five years from the expiration or prescription of the sentence, an identical or similar offense;

˗ already been definitively convicted for a minor offense, commits within the period of one year from the expiration or prescription of the sentence, the same minor offense.

###### Article 218-4 :

The offenses of theft, fraud, breach of trust, abuse of blank signature, receiving stolen goods, misappropriation of public funds or seized objects, extortion, swindling, corruption, extortion by public official, offenses relating to checks and infractions in company matters are considered as the same offense for the purposes of recidivism.

###### Article 218-5 :

In matters of minor offenses, there is recidivism when a first judgment that has become definitive has been rendered against the offender in the preceding twelve months.

###### Article 218-6 :

In case of concurrent aggravating circumstances and mitigating circumstances, the rules of mitigating circumstances are applied to the penalty resulting from the aggravating circumstances.

In case of concurrent mitigating circumstances and a legal excuse, the rules of mitigating circumstances are applied to the penalty resulting from the admission of the excuse.

If mitigating circumstances are admitted for a recidivist, the penalty resulting from the recidivism must first be fixed, before reducing it by reason of the mitigating circumstances.

##### CHAPTER 9: EXTINCTION AND ERASURE OF SENTENCE

###### Article 219-1:

A person who has attempted to commit a crime or an offense is, in the cases provided by law, exempt from penalty if, having warned the administrative or judicial authority, he or she has enabled the commission of the offense to be avoided and, where applicable, the co-authors or accomplices to be identified.

In the cases provided by law, the duration of the custodial sentence incurred by a person who has committed a crime or an offense is reduced if, having warned the administrative or judicial authority, he or she has enabled the offense to cease, the offense to be prevented from producing harm, or the other authors or accomplices to be identified.

The provisions of the preceding paragraph are also applicable when the person has enabled either the commission of a related offense of the same nature as the crime or offense for which he or she was prosecuted to be avoided, or such an offense to cease, to be prevented from producing harm, or its authors or accomplices to be identified.

###### Article 219-2:

Sentences pronounced for a crime are prescribed after twenty full years from the date on which the conviction decision became final, unless the law provides otherwise.

###### Article 219-3:

Sentences pronounced for an offense are prescribed after five full years from the date on which the conviction decision became final.

###### Article 219-4:

Sentences pronounced for a contravention are prescribed after three full years from the date on which the conviction decision became final.

###### Article 219-5:

Civil obligations resulting from a final criminal decision are prescribed according to the rules of the civil code.

###### Article 219-6:

Pardon only entails dispensation from execution of the sentence.

Pardon does not bar the right of the victim to obtain compensation for the harm caused by the offense.

###### Article 219-7:

Amnesty erases the convictions pronounced. It entails, without giving rise to restitution, the remission of all sentences. It restores the author or accomplice of the offense to the benefit of the suspended sentence that may have been granted upon a prior conviction.

Amnesty does not prejudice the rights of third parties.

###### Article 219-8:

Any person who, in the exercise of his or her duties, has knowledge of criminal convictions, disciplinary or professional sanctions, or prohibitions, disqualifications and incapacities erased by amnesty is prohibited from recalling their existence in any form whatsoever or allowing mention of them to remain in any document.

However, the original judgments, rulings and decisions are exempt from this prohibition. Furthermore, amnesty does not bar the execution of publication ordered as compensation.

###### Article 219-9:

Any person subject to a criminal, correctional or contravention sentence may benefit either from automatic rehabilitation under the conditions provided in this section, or from judicial rehabilitation granted under the conditions provided by the code of criminal procedure.

###### Article 219-10:

Rehabilitation is automatically acquired by the natural person convicted who has not, within the time periods determined below, incurred any new conviction to a criminal or correctional sentence:

˗ for conviction to a fine, after a period of three years from the day of payment of the fine, expiration of judicial constraint, or completed prescription;

˗ for a single conviction either to imprisonment not exceeding one year, or to a sentence other than life imprisonment, fixed-term imprisonment, or fine, after a period of five years from either the execution of the sentence or completed prescription; ˗ for a single conviction to imprisonment not exceeding ten years or for multiple convictions to imprisonment the total of which does not exceed five years, after a period of ten years from either the expiration of the sentence served or completed prescription. The periods provided in this article are doubled when the person has been convicted for acts committed in a state of legal recidivism.

Where convictions include suspended sentences in whole or in part, the rehabilitation periods run, for each of these convictions and including in the case of multiple convictions, from the date on which the conviction is set aside.

###### Article 219-11:

Rehabilitation is automatically acquired by the legal person convicted who has not, within the time periods determined below, incurred any new conviction to a criminal or correctional sentence:

˗ for conviction to a fine, after a period of five years from the day of payment of the fine or completed prescription;

˗ for conviction to a sentence other than a fine or dissolution, after a period of five years from either the execution of the sentence or completed prescription.

The periods provided in this article are doubled when the person has been convicted for acts committed in a state of legal recidivism.

Where a conviction includes a suspended sentence, the rehabilitation periods run from the date on which the conviction is set aside.

###### Article 219-12:

Sentences for which merger has been granted are considered as constituting a single sentence for the application of the provisions of the two preceding articles.

###### Article 219-13:

Rehabilitation produces the same effects as those provided by the articles relating to amnesty and pardon.

It erases all incapacities and disqualifications resulting from the conviction.

However, when the person has been sentenced to socio-judicial supervision or to the sentence of prohibition from exercising a professional or voluntary activity involving regular contact with minors, rehabilitation only produces its effects at the end of the measure.

Rehabilitation does not prohibit the taking into account of the conviction, by the judicial authorities alone, in case of new prosecutions, for the application of the rules on legal recidivism.

#### TITLE II: SECURITY MEASURES AND EDUCATIONAL MEASURES

##### CHAPTER 1: SECURITY MEASURES

###### Article 221-1:

Security measures are individual coercive measures imposed on individuals dangerous to social order in order to prevent offenses that their condition makes probable.

The following constitute security measures:

˗ confinement of the insane;

˗ treatment of persons addicted to narcotics;

˗ treatment of dangerous alcoholics;

˗ measures concerning certain persons suffering from psychic or neuropsychic disorder;

˗ supervised liberty for minors.

##### CHAPTER 2: EDUCATIONAL MEASURES

###### Article 222-1:

Educational measures are individual measures that tend toward the education, re-education, guardianship or assistance in favor of the convicted person.

Educational measures are applicable to minors under eighteen years of age found guilty of crimes or offenses.

They consist of:

˗ return of the minor to his or her family;

˗ placement of the minor with a relative or trustworthy person;

˗ placement of the minor in a charitable, religious or private institution;

˗ placement of the minor in a specialized public establishment.

## BOOK III: CRIMES AND OFFENSES AGAINST THE PUBLIC INTEREST

#### TITLE I: CRIMES AND OFFENSES AGAINST THE SECURITY OF THE STATE

##### CHAPTER 1: TREASON AND ESPIONAGE

###### Article 311-1:

Is guilty of treason and punished by life imprisonment, any Burkinabe who:

˗ bears arms against the State;

˗ maintains intelligence with a foreign power with a view to inducing it to undertake hostilities against Burkina Faso or provides it with the means to do so, either by facilitating the penetration of foreign forces into Burkinabe territory, or by undermining the loyalty of the armed forces, or in any other manner;

˗ delivers to a foreign power or its agents, either troops, or territories, cities, works, posts, warehouses, arsenals, buildings, equipment, ammunition, ships, aircraft or railway locomotives belonging to Burkina Faso or assigned to its defense;

˗ with a view to harming national defense, destroys or damages a ship, an aircraft or railway locomotive, equipment, supplies, construction or any installation, or who, with the same purpose, introduces into them, either before or after their completion, defects likely to damage them, to prevent them from functioning normally or to cause an accident.

###### Article 311-2:

Is guilty of treason and punished by a sentence of life imprisonment, any Burkinabe who, in time of war:

˗ incites military personnel to enter the service of a foreign power or facilitates the means for them to do so;

˗ carries out enlistments for a foreign power;

˗ maintains intelligence with a foreign power or with an agent, with a view to favoring the undertakings of this foreign power against Burkina Faso;

˗ obstructs the circulation of military means or equipment;

˗ knowingly participates in an enterprise of demoralization of the armed forces or populations aimed at harming national defense.

###### Article 311-3:

Is guilty of treason and punished by a sentence of life imprisonment any Burkinabe who, with a view to favoring a foreign power, obtains, delivers, destroys or allows to be destroyed in any form or by any means whatsoever any information, object, document or process that must be kept secret in the interest of national defense.

###### Article 311-4:

Is guilty of espionage and punished by a sentence of imprisonment of thirty years, any foreigner or stateless person who commits one of the acts provided for in Articles 311-1 and 311-3 above.

Is guilty of espionage and punished by a sentence of life imprisonment, any foreigner or stateless person who commits one of the acts provided for in Article 311-2 above.

###### Article 311-5:

Is punished, as a crime, the provocation to commit or the offer to commit one of the crimes referred to in this chapter.

###### Article 311-6:

Is punished, in time of war, by a sentence of imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to eight million (8,000,000) CFA francs and in time of peace, by a sentence of imprisonment of one year to five years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to four million (4,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, having complete knowledge of a project or date of treason or espionage, as to the nature of which he could not be mistaken, does not report it to the administrative, military or judicial authorities, from the moment he became aware of it.

###### Article 311-7:

Is punished by the same penalties, anyone who, being in contact with an individual exercising an activity likely to harm the security of the State, does not warn the authorities referred to in the preceding article from the moment he was able to become aware of this activity.

###### Article 311-8:

Are exempted from the penalties provided for against the perpetrators of conspiracies or other crimes against the security of the State, those perpetrators who, before any execution or attempted execution of these conspiracies or these crimes and before any proceedings have commenced, give first knowledge of them to the government, the administrative or judicial police authorities or who, even since the commencement of proceedings, facilitate the arrest of said perpetrators and accomplices.

##### CHAPTER 2: OTHER OFFENSES AGAINST NATIONAL DEFENSE

###### Article 312-1:

Is punished by life imprisonment, anyone who gathers, with the intention of delivering them to a foreign power, information, objects, documents or processes whose gathering and exploitation are likely to harm national defense.

###### Article 312-2:

Is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of one year to ten years and a fine of two hundred fifty thousand (250,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs, any custodian, any depositary by function or by capacity of information, object, document, or process that must be kept secret in the interest of national defense or whose knowledge may lead to the discovery of a national defense secret who, through clumsiness, imprudence, inattention or non-observance of regulations:

˗ destroys it, removes it, allows it to be destroyed or removed, reproduces it or allows it to be reproduced;

˗ brings it or allows it to be brought to the knowledge of the public or of an unqualified person.

###### Article 312-3:

Is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of one year to ten years and a fine of two hundred fifty thousand (250,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs anyone who, for a purpose other than that of delivering it to a foreign power or its agents:

˗ commits one of the offenses provided for in Article 312-2 above;

˗ secures possession of information, object, document or process kept secret in the interest of national defense or whose knowledge may lead to the discovery of a military secret.

###### Article 312-4:

Is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years and a fine of two hundred fifty thousand (250,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, knowingly and without prior authorization from the competent authority, delivers or communicates to a person acting on behalf of a power or a foreign enterprise, either an invention concerning national defense, or information, studies or manufacturing processes relating to an invention of this kind or to an industrial application concerning national defense.

###### Article 312-5:

Is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of one year to five years and a fine of two hundred fifty thousand (250,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs anyone who, without intent of treason or espionage, brings to the knowledge of an unqualified person or of the public, information not made public by the competent authority and whose disclosure is manifestly likely to harm national defense.

###### Article 312-6:

Are notably deemed to be national defense secrets for the application of this code:

- intelligence of a military, diplomatic, economic or industrial nature which, by its nature, must be known only to persons qualified to hold it and must, in the interest of national defense, be kept secret from any other person;
- objects, materials, writings, drawings, plans, maps, surveys, photographs or other productions and all other documents whatsoever which, by their nature, must be known only to persons qualified to handle or hold them, and must be kept secret from any other person as they may lead to the discovery of intelligence belonging to one of the categories mentioned above;
- military information of any nature not made public by the government and not included in the above enumerations whose publication, dissemination, disclosure or reproduction has been prohibited by law;
- intelligence relating either to measures taken to discover and arrest the perpetrators and accomplices of crimes or offenses against the external security of the State, or to the conduct of prosecutions and investigations, or to debates before the trial court.

###### Article 312-7:

Is punished by imprisonment from five years to ten years, and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs, whoever:

˗ enters under a disguise or a false name or by concealing their status or nationality into a work, post or arsenal, in the works, camps, bivouacs or cantonments of an army, in a warship or merchant vessel employed by national defense, in a military vehicle, in an establishment or worksite working for national defense;

˗ organizes, even without disguise or without concealing their name, status or nationality, in a covert manner, any means of correspondence or remote transmission likely to harm national defense;

˗ voluntarily flies over Burkinabè territory by means of a foreign aircraft without being authorized to do so by convention or by Burkinabè authority;

˗ executes in a prohibited zone fixed by military authority without its authorization, drawings, photographs, surveys or topographic operations inside or around places, works, posts or establishments of interest to national defense;

˗ stays in defiance of a prohibition within a determined radius around the works enumerated in the preceding points;

˗ communicates to an unqualified person or makes public intelligence relating either to measures taken to discover or arrest the perpetrators and accomplices of crimes or offenses defined in chapters 1 and 2 of this title, or to the conduct of proceedings, or to debates or deliberations before trial courts.

However, in peacetime, the offenses provided for in dashes 3, 4, 5 and 6 of this article are punished by imprisonment from one year to five years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to one million five hundred thousand (1,500,000) CFA francs.

###### Article 312-8:

Is punished by imprisonment from one year to ten years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs, whoever: ˗ by hostile acts not approved by the competent authority, exposes Burkina Faso to a declaration of war or to reprisals;

˗ by acts not approved by the competent authority, exposes Burkinabè to suffering reprisals;

˗ maintains with the agents of a foreign power intelligence likely to harm the military or diplomatic situation of Burkina Faso or its essential economic interests.

###### Article 312-9:

Is punished by imprisonment from eleven years to twenty-one years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, whoever, in time of war, directly or through an intermediary and in defiance of enacted prohibitions, engages in acts of commerce or maintains correspondence or relations that may harm national defense with subjects or agents of an enemy power.

###### Article 312-10:

Is punished by imprisonment from one year to ten years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, whoever, in peacetime, hinders the circulation of military means or materials or by any means whatsoever, provokes, facilitates or organizes a violent or concerted action with a view to harming national defense.

###### Article 312-11:

Is punished by imprisonment from one year to ten years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, whoever, in peacetime, knowingly participates in an enterprise of demoralization of the Armed Forces likely to harm national defense.

###### Article 312-12:

Is punished by imprisonment from one year to ten years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, whoever, in peacetime, in Burkina Faso and clandestinely, recruits or trains with a view to their recruitment persons called upon to bear arms on behalf of a foreign power or organized group.

###### Article 312-13:

The attempt of the offenses provided for in this section is punishable.

##### CHAPTER 3: ATTACKS, CONSPIRACIES AND OTHER

OFFENSES AGAINST THE SECURITY OF THE STATE

###### Article 313-1:

There is conspiracy as soon as the resolution to act is concerted and agreed upon between two or more persons with a view to:

˗ changing the legal regime by violence;

˗ inciting populations to take up arms against the legal authority of the State or to take up arms against one another;

˗ undermining the integrity of national territory;

˗ organizing massacre and devastation.

The applicable penalty is imprisonment from eleven years to twenty-one years.

###### Article 313-2:

Conspiracy followed by an act committed or begun to prepare its execution is an attack punished by imprisonment from eleven years to thirty years.

###### Article 313-3:

Is punished by imprisonment from one year to ten years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, whoever makes an unagreed proposal to form a conspiracy to commit one of the crimes provided for in Article 313-1 above.

###### Article 313-4:

In all cases, the court seized may furthermore pronounce the interdiction of civil rights for a duration which cannot exceed five years.

###### Article 313-5:

Is punished by life imprisonment, whoever, without having been legally invested or who, without legitimate reason, takes any military command or who, against the order of the legitimate authority, retains such command.

Commanders who keep their army or troop assembled after disbandment or separation has been ordered, are punished by the same penalty.

###### Article 313-6 :

Is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of three hundred thousand (300,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs anyone who, in time of war, being able to dispose of public force, requisitions or orders, causes to requisition or order the action or employment thereof to prevent the execution of texts on military recruitment or on mobilization.

If this requisition or this order is followed by effect, the guilty party is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of eleven years to thirty years.

Is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of one year to five years, and a fine of two hundred fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs anyone who, in time of peace, being able to dispose of public force requisitions or orders, causes to requisition or order the action or employment thereof to prevent the execution of texts on military recruitment or on mobilization. If this requisition or this order is followed by effect, the guilty party is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of five years to ten years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.

##### CHAPITRE 4 : DES CRIMES COMMIS PAR LA PARTICIPATION A UN

MOUVEMENT DE DESTABILISATION

###### Article 314-1 :

Are punished by imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-one years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, those who, in an insurrectional movement:

˗ are found carrying weapons and ammunition;

˗ occupy or attempt to occupy public buildings or private properties;

˗ erect barricades;

˗ oppose by violence and threats the convocation or the assembly of public force;

˗ provoke or facilitate the gathering of insurgents by flags, rallying signs or any other means;

˗ intercept or attempt to intercept communications between depositaries of public force;

˗ break or attempt to break communication and telecommunication lines;

˗ seize weapons and ammunition by violence, or threat, pillage, disarming of agents of public force.

Are punished by life imprisonment, those who make use of their firearms.

###### Article 314-2 :

Is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of eleven years to twenty-one years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who sets fire to or destroys by explosive device buildings, stores, arsenals or other properties belonging to the State.

If death has followed, the perpetrator is punished by a penalty of life imprisonment.

###### Article 314-3 :

Is punished by a penalty of life imprisonment, anyone who, whether to invade domains or properties of the State, cities, posts, stores, arsenals, or to pillage and share public funds, public or national properties or those of a community, or to make attack or resistance against public force acting against the perpetrators of these crimes, places himself at the head of armed bands or exercises therein any command function whatsoever.

The same penalty applies to those who direct the association, raise or cause to raise, organize or cause to organize the bands or knowingly provide or procure for them weapons, ammunition and instruments of crime or send supply convoys or practice in any other manner intelligence with the leaders of the bands.

###### Article 314-4 :

In the case where one or more of the crimes mentioned in this section have been executed or simply attempted by a band, life imprisonment applies to all individuals forming part of the band and who were seized at the place of seditious assembly.

Is punished by the same penalty, although not seized at the place, anyone who directed the sedition or exercised in the band any command whatsoever.

###### Article 314-5 :

No penalty is pronounced for act of sedition against those who, having formed part of these bands without exercising command therein, withdraw at the first warning from civil or military authorities or even those who were seized outside the places of seditious assembly without opposing resistance and without weapons.

##### CHAPITRE 5 : DES ATTROUPEMENTS

###### Article 315-1 :

Are prohibited, the formation of armed gatherings on the public highway as well as unarmed gatherings which are of a nature to disturb public tranquility.

The gathering is deemed armed when several of the individuals who compose it are carriers of visible or hidden weapons or when a single one of these individuals, carrier of visible weapons, is not immediately expelled from the gathering by those very ones who form part of it.

###### Article 315-2 :

All persons who form gatherings in public places or on the public highway are required to disperse at the summons of the authorities responsible for maintaining order.

If the gathering does not disperse, the summons is renewed twice. If the three summons have remained without effect or even in the case where after a first summons or a second, it is not possible to make the second or the third, force may be employed.

###### Article 315-3 :

Public force may be employed without summons:

˗ if violence or assault is exercised against law enforcement forces;

˗ if law enforcement forces cannot otherwise defend the ground they occupy or the persons and posts under their guard.

###### Article 315-4 :

Anyone having formed part of an armed gathering which disperses upon customary summons, is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of six months to one year and a fine of two hundred fifty thousand (250,000) to six hundred thousand (600,000) CFA francs.

If the gathering is formed by night, the penalty of imprisonment is one year to three years and the fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs.

Nevertheless, no penalty is pronounced for gathering against those who, having formed part of it without being personally armed, withdraw upon the first summons.

###### Article 315-5 :

Is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of two years to five years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs anyone who forms part of an armed gathering which disperses only after employment of public force.

The penalty of imprisonment is five years to ten years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to four million (4,000,000) CFA francs, if the armed gathering was dispersed only after public force made use of its weapons.

###### Article 315-6 :

The aggravation of penalties provided for in article 315-5 above is not applicable to unarmed individuals forming part of an armed gathering, in the case of hidden weapons, except when they had knowledge of the presence in the gathering of several persons carrying these weapons.

Those who did not have this knowledge incur the penalties provided for in article 315-4 above.

###### Article 315-7 :

In the cases provided for in article 315-5 above, the seized jurisdiction may furthermore pronounce the prohibition of exercise of civil rights for a duration which cannot exceed five years.

###### Article 315-8 :

Is punished as the crime or the offense according to the distinctions established in the preceding articles, any provocation followed by effect, to an armed or unarmed gathering by speeches publicly uttered or by writings or printed materials posted or distributed.

Printers, engravers, lithographers, posters and distributors are punished as accomplices, when they act knowingly.

Provocation not followed by effect is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of two months to six months.

###### Article 315-9 :

Prosecutions directed for crimes and offense of unlawful assembly do not prevent the prosecution of crimes and offenses which are committed in the midst of these gatherings.

###### Article 315-10 :

In all cases, confiscation of weapons and ammunition must be ordered.

##### CHAPITRE 6 : WEAPONS AND AMMUNITION

###### Article 316-1 :

Anyone who, without legally required authorization, manufactures, exports, imports, possesses, transfers, sells or purchases a firearm or ammunition or acts as an intermediary in the sale or purchase or is responsible for the transport, transit through the territory of Burkina Faso or transhipment of a firearm or ammunition, is punishable by imprisonment from one year to ten years and a fine from one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Article 316-2 :

Anyone who lends a firearm or ammunition to a person without ensuring that the person is authorized to possess a firearm or ammunition is considered an accomplice.

###### Article 316-3 :

In all cases, confiscation of the weapon or ammunition is mandatory. In case of recidivism, the court may order the closure of the establishment in question either temporarily for a period that cannot exceed five years, or permanently.

###### Article 316-4 :
